The patient reports new-onset low mood, anxiety, social withdrawal, and functional impairment beginning shortly after a significant stressful event four months ago, with symptoms persisting for nearly that duration. The absence of prior mood or anxiety symptoms supports a reaction to the stressor.     "role": "user",
    "content": "Candidate Diseases: \n[\"Major Depressive Disorder\", \"Adjustment Disorder\"]\n\nInterview Transcript: \nDoctor: Hello, I'm Dr. Smith. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hello, Dr. Smith. I'm seeking help because I've been really struggling to cope lately after going through a very stressful event. I've been feeling a mix of low mood and constant anxiety, and it's making it hard to get through the day.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me when this stressful event happened and how long ago it was?\n\nPatient: The stressful event happened about four or five months ago. My symptoms started pretty much right after it, and they have been going on for the past four months.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced symptoms like low mood or anxiety almost every day during these past four months?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've been experiencing both almost every single day for the last four months. It feels like a constant mix of deep sadness and hopelessness, combined with a persistent worry and nervousness that I just can't seem to shake off.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in most activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I've been so overwhelmed by the anxiety and low mood that I haven't really thought about whether I've lost interest in things.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to whether you still find enjoyment in activities like hobbies, socializing, or things you used to look forward to, or has that changed since the stressful event?\n\nPatient: Yes, now that I think about it, I've really stopped socializing and doing my hobbies. I've mostly been withdrawing from my friends and staying home because I just feel too overwhelmed to face anyone. It has definitely changed quite a bit since the stressful event.\n\nDoctor: How much has this affected your ability to function in daily life, such as at work, school, or in relationships?\n\nPatient: Honestly, it has been quite difficult to manage my day-to-day things lately. I'm not entirely sure of the exact extent of how much it's affecting my work or relationships, but everything just feels a lot harder to get through.\n\nDoctor: Can you give me an example of how your mood or anxiety has interfered with your work, daily responsibilities, or relationships since the stressful event?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to give a specific example of how it's interfered. I just feel very overwhelmed in general, and I've been keeping to myself a lot lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble meeting work or household responsibilities, or have people close to you expressed concern about your withdrawal or mood changes?\n\nPatient: Yes, my family has definitely expressed concern because I've been withdrawing from everyone so much lately. Over the past four months, I've just been struggling constantly with these mood changes, feeling really anxious and down. It has made it quite difficult to keep up with my usual responsibilities at home.\n\nDoctor: Before this stressful event four months ago, did you have any history of prolonged low mood, anxiety, or similar symptoms?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall having any significant history of low mood or anxiety before this stressful event started four months ago. I generally felt like I was coping well with life and didn't have these kinds of struggles.\n\nDoctor: In addition to low mood and loss of interest, have you experienced other symptoms nearly every day, such as significant weight change, sleep disturbances, fatigue, feelings of worthlessness, or difficulty concentrating?\n\nPatient: I haven't really experienced things like significant weight changes, sleep issues, or feelings of worthlessness. I'm not really sure about those other symptoms, as my main struggle has just been the constant anxiety and low mood."
  }

{"diagnosis": "Adjustment Disorder", "candidates": ["Major Depressive Disorder", "Adjustment Disorder"], "reason": "The patient developed persistent low mood and anxiety within three months of a significant stressful event, with symptoms lasting for four months and impairing social and occupational functioning. The absence of classic depressive features such as significant weight change, sleep disturbance, worthlessness, or fatigue, along with the lack of prior history, supports Adjustment Disorder over Major Depressive Disorder."}
